38: 
39: Now choose any \(2p\) positions in increasing original order. Let \(C\) count selected reflections and \(n=2p-C\) selected rotations. Direct multiplication under
40: \[
41: (v,e)(v',e')=(v+ev',ee')
42: \]
43: shows that a selected rotation of value \(x\) contributes
44: \[
45: (-1)^{\#\{\text{selected preceding reflections}\}}x.
46: \]
47: Each selected \(\sigma_0\) contributes zero to the first coordinate and reverses the sign. Skipped reflections do neither.
48: 
49: All selected rotations from \(U\) therefore contribute \(+1\). If \(v\) selected rotations contribute effectively \(-1\), the product is
50: \[
51: (n-2v\bmod p,\;(-1)^C).
52: \]
53: This accounts for every reflection-value term: those terms are zero by construction.  
54: **Location:** \(P1\) LF423–435; manuscript original LF51–63.
55: 
56: The selection cases are exhaustive:
57: 
58: 1. **\(C\) odd.** Its second coordinate is \(-1\), so the product cannot be identity.
59: 
60: 2. **\(C=0\).** Identity requires
61:    \[
62:    2p-2v\equiv0\pmod p.
63:    \]
64:    Since \(p\) is odd, the possible negative counts within \(0\le v\le2p\) are \(0,p,2p\). The latter two exceed \(B=p-1\); \(v=0\) would require \(2p\) positive rotations, exceeding \(A=2p-1\).  
65:    **Location:** \(P1\) LF469–475; manuscript original LF97–103.
66: 
67: 3. **\(C=2m>0\).** Because \(F=p\) and \(p\) is odd,
68:    \[
69:    2m\le p-1,\qquad n=2p-2m
70:    \]
71:    is even and less than \(2p\).
72: 
73:    Label selected reflections \(f_{i_1},\ldots,f_{i_{2m}}\) in original order. For each \(a=1,\ldots,m\), the open interval between \(f_{i_{2a-1}}\) and \(f_{i_{2a}}\) contains at least \(t_{i_{2a-1}}\). Its index exists because \(i_{2a-1}<i_{2a}\le p\).
74: 
75:    Exactly \(2a-1\) selected reflections precede every rotation in this interval. Therefore each negative-valued rotation there would contribute \(+1\) if selected. Unselected intervening reflections do not change this parity.
76: 
77:    These \(m\) intervals are disjoint, so at least \(m\) distinct negative-valued occurrences cannot provide effective negative contributions. Since \(U\) cannot provide any,
78:    \[
79:    v\le p-1-m.
80:    \]
81:    Skipping interval rotations does not enlarge this capacity.
82: 
83:    Identity would require the even integer \(n-2v\), with
84:    \[
85:    |n-2v|\le n<2p,
86:    \]
87:    to be divisible by odd \(p\). The only even multiple of \(p\) in that range is zero. Hence identity requires
88:    \[
89:    v=n/2=p-m,
90:    \]
91:    exceeding capacity by exactly one.  
92:    **Location:** \(P1\) LF439–465; manuscript original LF67–93.
93: 
94: There is no \(C=2p\) branch because only \(p\) reflections exist. The argument retains the original cut throughout, uses no cyclic transport or independent sign choice, and covers every increasing-index selection.
95: 
96: Therefore, for **every** stated prime, an actual word inside the frozen domain avoids every ordered \(2p\)-term identity product. This establishes the falsity of the **whole universal implication**. It leaves other residual words unclassified.  
97: **Location:** \(P1\) LF479–493; manuscript original LF107–121.
98:

30: 1. **Group products and order are correct — proof LF17–36.**  
31:    The multiplication law gives the prefix-sign formula directly. An even-reflection selection has rotational product. For even-size selections,
32:    \[

37: 2. **The group algebra and ideal nilpotence follow from the written algebra — proof LF40–72.**  
38:    The basis multiplication makes \(A=\mathbb F_p[(\mathbb F_p,+)^2]\) a commutative algebra of dimension \(p^2\). Its generators satisfy \(X^p=Y^p=1\); characteristic \(p\) therefore gives
39:    \[
40:    (X-1)^p=(Y-1)^p=0.
41:    \]
42:    The resulting map
43:    \[
44:    \mathbb F_p[S,T]/(S^p,T^p)\longrightarrow A
45:    \]
46:    is surjective because \(X=1+s\) and \(Y=1+t\) generate every basis element. The quotient has the \(p^2\) monomial basis \(S^iT^j\), \(0\le i,j<p\), so the map is an isomorphism.
47: 
48:    Augmentation sets \(s=t=0\), hence \(J=(s,t)\). Every monomial of total degree at least \(2p-1\) has an exponent at least \(p\), proving
49:    \[
50:    J^{2p-1}=0.
51:    \]
52:    Also \(E_{(a,h)}\equiv1+as+ht\pmod{J^2}\), and independence of \(1,s,t\) modulo \(J^2\) proves the three-moment membership criterion. No matrix or experimental output enters this reasoning. The optional nonvanishing \(J^{2p-2}\ne0\) also follows algebraically from the surviving basis monomial \(s^{p-1}t^{p-1}\); TILE does not need it.

54: 3. **Both unit constructions and GOOD are correct — proof LF76–140.**  
55:    The RR and FF elements have augmentation zero, empty-option coefficient \(1\), and the stated actual subset interpretations.
56: 
57:    For a balanced four-block, the admissible subsets are exactly empty, its two rotations, its two reflections, and all four positions. The three moment equations are
58:    \[
59:    1+u+v+w=0,\qquad u+v+2w=0,\qquad au+bv+cw=0.
60:    \]
61:    They force \(w=1\), \(u+v=-2\), and \(au+bv=-c\). Consequently solvability is exactly
62:    \[
63:    a\ne b\quad\text{or}\quad c=2a.
64:    \]
65:    When \(a\ne b\), the displayed coefficients
66:    \[
67:    u=\frac{2b-c}{a-b},\qquad v=\frac{c-2a}{a-b}
68:    \]
69:    satisfy both equations, with division only by the explicitly nonzero denominator. When \(a=b,c=2a\), \(u=-2,v=0,w=1\) works. The remaining case is inconsistent.
70:

73:    | Type order | Actual \(c\) |
74:    |---|---|

78:    | FRRF | \(b-a\) |
